# Development status

## October 10 · Erudite Standing Stone trade-off

The owner approved replacing Erudite’s strictly better XP discount with **75% of the ordinary unrounded budget in XP plus one-third in mana**, e.g. 24 XP becomes 18 XP + 8 mana. The exchanged XP portion carries an explicit 4/3 mana premium. Both resources use the shared typed transaction, affordability and rollback, and appear side by side in the native menu. Existing Erudite route IDs and shard keys remain unchanged. The revised payload is protocol 6; other route prices, cosmetics, recipes and network membership are unchanged.

**Verified:** 228 scoped unit tests (50 suites), 246 combined unit tests (53 suites), Java 21 builds and Kithkyn compatibility, and all **50 targeted Minecraft tests** with Kithkyn. World checks cover exact paired payments, either missing currency, XP callbacks canceling or removing mana, reentrancy, stale routes, canceled/redirected/throwing travel and both-resource/recovery-delay refunds. The shared payer now checks mana-spend success after XP callbacks. [Actual client evidence](art/standing-stone-erudite-native/README.md) shows both costs, independently unavailable currencies, refresh behavior and all 185 public/bound JEI lookups. Eighteen unedited native frames were captured; unchanged EMI recipe integration was not rerun.

The verified combined artifact is installed in **Kithkyn Testing** with an outside-mods backup; [the receipt](art/standing-stone-erudite-native/release-receipt.json) pins the exact hashes. Restart Minecraft to load it. The scoped publication excludes other agents’ equipment changes while the installed combined build retains them. Long-distance/resource-generation balance remains playtesting.

## October 10 · Standing Stone payment imbuements implemented

The owner approved the complete [five-route package](design/standing-stone-imbuement-plan.md). The Plinth carrying the Ender Pearl now selects ordinary XP, Erudite XP/mana, Charged mana, Fasting hunger or Bloodbound nonlethal health. Crafting copies the full verified shard key unchanged. Route data survives placement, either-half mining and saving separately from finish, silhouette, signature and editable endpoint name. Missing baseline route data keeps ordinary XP; malformed explicit routes reject access and remain malformed after mining.

Distance quotes use the unchanged XP budget and shared ResourceValuation conversions. Departure alone selects payment. The shared typed transaction restores the selected resource and mana recovery delay for canceled, redirected, failed, throwing or callback-invalidated trips; reentrant requests and stale route sessions reject. Exact health payment must leave life, hunger may reach zero and creative bypasses costs. Native six-row menus show real resource symbols and refresh affordability without reopening closed menus.

Dynamic JEI/EMI displays cover **180 primary variants plus five separate Chiseled Stone Bricks alternatives**. Both public and privately bound outputs index correctly without exposing the shard key. The ordered recipe has interchangeable quarter-turns; because its masonry pair is identical, a reflection is also the same valid pattern.

**Verified:** scoped publication build and Kithkyn compatibility; **227 unit tests across 50 suites**, **all 48 targeted Minecraft tests with Kithkyn and all 399 required tests in the complete standalone suite**, adjective-generation drift checks and whitespace checks. World evidence includes 740 rotated recipe checks, all 180 finish/route placement/mining/save combinations, all five actual ritual outputs, changed-socket cancellation, survival resource debits, strict health boundaries, creative, malformed/unbound drops, typed payload round trips, reentrant XP callbacks and canceled/redirected/throwing teleports. The combined development build additionally passed **244 unit tests across 53 suites** and the final 48-test pass; an earlier broader pass covered all 67 required tests including Cinderweave integration.

[Actual client evidence](art/standing-stone-imbuements-native/README.md) contains funded/unfunded menus for every route and all five recipe screens in JEI and EMI. Both viewers verify all 185 generic/bound recipes; the final JEI inspection also checks closed-screen refresh behavior. No new model or texture is introduced. The verified combined build is installed in Kithkyn Testing; [release hashes and backup receipt](art/standing-stone-imbuements-native/release-receipt.json) distinguish it from the scoped publication artifact. Restart Minecraft to load it. Initial long-distance balance, a real multiplayer/restart soak and existing unrelated EMI/temporary-water warnings remain follow-up; the combined development build was checked through targeted suites, while the scoped release passed the complete standalone world suite.

## October 9 · Fractious Fluxed Flint

The owner approved a stronger, riskier repair imbuement. **Magma Block beneath the Flint offering** selects Fractious during construction: **50% target-max durability per activation and 20% intrinsic Flint backfire chance**, with unchanged total budget and exactly one Flint point spent per restored point. Missing damage and remaining budget still cap the actual transfer. The choice composes with Stabilized/Reinforced in all eight sets, including Restive, Audacious and Impetuous full-set names. Exact saved target/catalyst components, old variant identities, public recipe IDs and the shared independent-input failure rule are preserved.

**Verified:** **217 model tests across 47 suites, all 376 required Minecraft tests with Kithkyn, packaging and compatibility**, plus **13 native assertions and five inspected client frames**. Eight rotated construction patterns, three retained selectors, forty-point transfer, all cap/damage/budget limits, exact save/load and higher-risk failure are covered. Naming/Spellshaping checks and 490 leyline fixtures pass. Seven changed packaged classes and the loaded Flint assets match tested files. The cleanup assertion was corrected to run after the existing explosion animation; an interrupted rerun is distinct from the final complete pass. [Evidence and hashes](verification/fractious-flint-2026-10-09/README.md).

The update is installed in **Kithkyn Testing** with a verified backup and preserves the Hourglass chat's existing texture preview; all release classes/data match the installed package. ## October 8 · Wayfarer Boots, crafted variants and native readiness

The owner approved the complete [Wayfarer package](design/wayfarer-imbuement-review.md). It is now implemented: a real grounded sprint-jump pays five mana for a three-second +20% movement burst and one landing's four-HP protection, with fifteen-second wearer/ability recovery. Time/Motion/Amplify resolve through the shared runtime. Swift, Enduring, Reinforced and Quickened supply sixteen trusted, single-adjective variants; all have ordered eight-seat rituals and viewer entries. Normal/protected-hit wear, 65/98 durability, Laced Thread repairs and the existing exclusion of Unbreaking/Mending are supported. Copy/variant swapping preserves recovery; same-tick unequip revokes active benefits.

The selected Feather Tabs direction now has a true 16×16 sprite and native worn atlas. [Actual client artwork, names and animation](art/wayfarer-native/README.md) show registered boots beside vanilla items, worn poses, exact plain/Nimble/Unfaltering tooltips and real private mana/recovery packets. Both vanilla-white layers clear independently, with compounded overlap. Loaded hashes match production. Existing robe behavior and pixels are preserved.

**Verified:** 63 focused equipment/runtime unit tests across nine suites; expanded native run **22/22** (ten boots plus twelve robes) with Kithkyn; final native presentation run and 76 original animation frames. The final combined source, including both slot hooks and mounted/flying/native-water assertions, subsequently passed **202 unit tests across 45 suites and all 354 required Minecraft tests with Kithkyn**, plus packaging. [Shipment verification](verification/wayfarer-adjectives-shipment-2026-10-08/README.md) records that final run. [Receipt, exact hashes and limitations](verification/wayfarer-2026-10-08/README.md). **Remaining clothing:** Wardweave and Cinderweave have working shared-trait abilities, durability, crafting and enchantment policy, but their crafted imbuement variants still need authored routes and review. Dawnsight Hood, Patchwork Robes and Spiderstep Boots still need final mechanics and implementation. Naming coverage alone does not complete an item's mechanics.

## October 8 · shared mana equivalent revised to thirty

The owner revised the shared ratio to **1 full heart = 2 full hunger icons = 30 XP points = 30 mana**, superseding the earlier forty-mana revision. [Canonical payment values](design/resource-payments.md), AGENTS routing and generated Spellshaping text now agree. `ResourceValuation` preserves the fractional **7.5 mana per food point** until conversion rounding; Bloodbound/Fasting inherit current rates. Homebound Eye's mana route now spends thirty mana plus two wear, while its health/hunger/XP routes retain one heart / two hunger icons / thirty XP. Stored routes, keys, mana regeneration and base spell prices are unchanged.

The [hourglass imbuement draft](design/kairotic-hourglass.md) now exchanges up to thirty mana, capped at 75% of the adjusted price. At baseline, its proposed alternate routes cost thirty mana plus one heart / two hunger icons / thirty XP. Its accepted sixty-mana / fifteen-second / ten-use unmodified baseline remains unchanged, and neither the item nor its draft imbuements is implemented by this pass.

**Verified:** Java 21 build and Kithkyn compatibility, **189 unit tests** across 43 suites, **11 required Minecraft tests**, all 56 Spellshaping authoring checks and seventeen Python balance tests. Includes fractional-food boundary rounding, mixed exchanges, Eye prices/refusals, canceled XP/dimension travel and refunds, atomic crafting/payment and unchanged recovery. The initial world run had one stale old-price travel assertion; correcting its expected remaining mana from sixty to seventy resolved it. ## October 8 · Kairotic Hourglass ingredients and baseline locked

The owner chose a position-return **Kairotic Hourglass**, activated by right-click. It records while carried in the player's inventory and returns to fifteen seconds earlier, falling back to the oldest available position while its history fills. The accepted unmodified baseline is **60 mana, 10 maximum durability and one wear per successful return**. Relative recipe shape matters, with whole rotations equivalent. Imbuements may adjust the return interval, trade mana for health/hunger/XP or adjust durability with trade-offs; exact selectors and formulas remain open. Health is more valuable than food, which is more valuable than experience. [The design](design/kairotic-hourglass.md) records the rolling server history, proposed lifecycle/arrival rules and required behavior tests. Cross-dimension returns remain an open choice. This supersedes the earlier speed-and-fatigue candidate; other researched items remain proposals.

The owner locked the four ingredients and baseline after reviewing the shaped four-slot clockwise pattern **Clock → Glass → Echo Shard → Ender Pearl**, preserving existing quarter-turn matching and distinct reflections. Echo's authored memory role is informed by vanilla Recovery Compass crafting and Iron's Timeless Slurry recipe; neither source establishes a universal time mineral. The generic Amethyst ingredient, shapeless matching and arbitrary Copper/Amethyst interval selectors were withdrawn. Existing Clock/Amethyst Enduring remains a specific 20% Time rule. The shared Bloodbound/Fasting equal mana exchange per heart/food point is reviewed in the audit above; no shipped conversion changed. Further hourglass work follows the requested shape/payment review.

**Verified:** the documentation distinguishes locked behavior/ingredients/baseline from proposed safeguards and unresolved shaping. Shared item resolution, typed payment, native mana, existing teleport cancellation, ritual matching, executable Spellshaping and the full pinned material-use reference were inspected for implementation fit. This pass records the design only; no new hourglass gameplay, item assets, gameplay tests, installed build or publication is claimed.

## October 8 · Fluxed Flint repair implementation

Fluxed Flint now provides the shared magical repair route. Offer one Flint and one damaged tagged magical item on any two available Plinths in a valid four- or eight-slot layout, leave all other available seats and center empty, and activate the Spellstone. Success returns a copy of the actual target with only its damage changed and leaves the worn catalyst in place; its final budget destroys it. All other data, including names, enchantments, inventories, identities, stored scrolls, shaping and Staff selection, is preserved. The initial recipe uses Flint, Diamond Block, Netherite Ingot and Echo Shard on any four available Plinths with an empty center; construction is available in the shared recipe viewers.

**Provisional playtest balance:** 128 total repair points, at most a quarter of the target's maximum durability per activation, one catalyst point per restored point, and Volatile 2 (10% inherent risk). Shared ritual volatility now rolls each participating input separately, stops at the first trigger and preserves that exact input while destroying the others, including a nontriggering reference. A Flint-triggered backfire loses the repair target and preserves the Flint without wear. Two-item vanilla combining is blocked for Flint, Wands and Staffs; existing robe anvil repairs and renaming remain available.

**Verified:** all 179 unit tests, packaging, Kithkyn version compatibility and all 329 required Minecraft world tests passed in the frozen combined source. A final focused run passed all thirteen Fluxed Flint world cases after adding reference-forfeit/reservation coverage; production code was unchanged from the full pass. Five unedited native client captures were inspected beside vanilla Flint, Echo Shard and Amethyst Shard, covering the new 16×16 sprite, held/offered item, repair lift, dropped output and final durability bars. Four integrated-client assertions and loaded-asset hashes passed. [Verification and staged build](verification/fluxed-flint-2026-10-08/README.md), [artwork and native views](art/fluxed-flint-v1/README.md), [mechanics and provisional recipe](design/magical-repair.md). Stabilizing imbuements, dynamic repair viewer entries, survival balance and owner artwork acceptance remain open. The testing pack and published branches were not changed by this implementation.

## October 7 · approved Wardweave and Cinderweave robes

The owner locked in the two robe packages after excluding Unbreaking/Mending and accepting durability as the continuing cost of uncapped trait growth. Both native chest items now provide 2 armor, zero toughness and +25 maximum mana, with 80 durability and matching-thread anvil repair of 20 per thread. Eight-offering rituals require four wool blocks of one color, two matching magical threads and the approved reagents; mixed colors reject, sockets remain, and ordinary dropped outputs commit atomically. Native dyeing retains fixed trim and other components. JEI/EMI share 32 concrete color-specific recipe entries.

Wardweave arms one 4-second / 2-HP ward after positive health damage, with wearer-owned 12-second recovery; Time and Force × Amplify use the shared immutable ability/binding runtime. Cinderweave resolves Fire × 25% and Amplify × 2 HP on each tagged fire hit. Robe protection runs after shields/hurt immunity and before armor, enchantments/resistance/absorption. Actual prevention charges that robe once, including full prevention and armor-bypassing fire/fall damage; ordinary unprotected hits retain vanilla wear. Removal/breakage revokes the ward and permanently clamps excess mana without a refill. Excluded enchantments are denied through table/anvil eligibility and filtered from gameplay, including injected enchantment components.

## October 7 · mundane Staff shafts

Seven physical Staffs now exist as ordinary crafting-table weapons: Stick, Bamboo, Bone, Blaze Rod, Breeze Rod, End Rod and Lightning Rod. Each recipe uses two matching vanilla materials in a vertical line. Their original 16×16 sprites now have broad material faces, leather grips and distinct physical finials. All share the wooden-pickaxe attack profile—2 total melee damage at 1.2 attacks per second—and spend one durability only on a successful left-click hit; their durability is 59 / 59 / 131 / 203 / 203 / 250 / 250 respectively. Holding right-click raises a 40% frontal guard after the normal five-tick delay and spends one durability per guarded hit. It deliberately skips the full shield's block, knockback and axe-disable behavior. Magical Staffs keep the same left-click profile; right-click guards only when their selected slot is empty, otherwise it attempts the selected spell. Any mundane Staff occupies the magical construction ritual's physical **Shaft** seat; no repair system was changed.

**Verified:** Java compilation, all Staff authoring checks, 179 JUnit tests and all **329** required Minecraft GameTests pass. The added world test checks the final guard behavior: a raised Staff reduces a frontal ten-damage hit to six, loses one durability and does not knock the attacker back. [The native item capture](art/mundane-staffs/README.md) records the seven rendered sprites. The construction ritual consumes the chosen mundane Staff and produces the existing generic magical Staff; shaft-specific magical models and bonuses remain future design work.

Wear commits once with initial payment before volatility; paid chaotic forfeits use the same amount. Failed payment and canceled preparation use none, and continuations add none. Empty menu rows retain a blank item square and a dimmed, disabled **Empty** button, with no active-selection change when clicked.

**Verified:** production build, **173 unit tests**, **22 focused Staff world tests**, **52 actual client checks**, both Staff authoring checks and Kithkyn platform compatibility passed. ## October 6 · Crane Bag shared Bundle implementation

**Crane Bag** now provides one server-owned, vanilla-weighted Bundle inventory per full inherited attunement key, shared across dimensions of the same server save. Secondary-clicks transfer between the real cursor/menu slot and that pool; right-click in hand drops shared contents once, retaining canceled spawns. Existing vanilla packets synchronize bounded display snapshots only when revisions change. Per-key locks reject reentrant transfers, item components remain exact and canceled/forged/repeated interactions cannot substitute cached contents for authoritative items. Destroying a physical bag retains its pool. The starting policy rejects bag/Bundle nesting, container-component items, oversized previews and read-only/output-only/fake slots.

The user explicitly deferred the survival recipe and final image. `vestige:crane_bag` uses temporary vanilla Bundle art; `/vestige_magic crane_bag` creates a bound test bag from a valid shard held in the main hand without consuming it. Its tooltip uses the common four-rune formatter beneath the name, followed by the native contents grid/fullness. The [design](design/crane-bag.md) records controls, persistence, synchronization, limits and follow-up decisions.

**Verified:** isolated Java 21 build and Kithkyn compatibility pass with **105 unit tests and all 11 focused Crane Bag Minecraft tests**, zero failures/errors/skips. Native tests cover two-player last-item competition, weighted and partial transfers, exact components, real click-packet claims/replays, changed bindings, refused slots/cursors, cross-dimension/stored previews, nesting/payload bounds, save/reload, malformed-file protection, destroyed bags and canceled/reentrant drops. Unreadable existing SavedData is preserved instead of being replaced with empty pools. The negative corruption fixture intentionally logs a loader error. A repeated-world fixture initially counted its retained prior diamond; resetting that reproducible fixture fixes the test. The full unrelated native suite was not repeated.

The first diagnostic exposed vanilla's image insertion before the rune line; the corrected view is the final evidence. This native GUI uses fixture snapshots, with server gameplay tested independently. Standing Stones now have **16 native faceted forms**: Blade, Shoulder, Leaning, Spire, Wedge, Crown, Fang, Cleft, Hunched, Taper, Slant, Tablet, Chisel, Saddle, Obelisk and Keel. The three approved bodies are retained byte for byte; the thirteen additions vary their outlines, crowns, lean and proportions. All forms support the same **36 masonry finishes**, giving 576 cosmetic combinations. Whole bodies use 17–25 baked faces, without exceeding the previous maximum. Per-form anchors fit the glowing signature and drifting rune glyphs to each face.

The Attunement Shard's complete signature determines the shape and rune marks. Two matching masonry block offerings determine the finish. Same full signatures share their same-dimension network regardless of finish; coincidentally matching visible forms or marks do not link distinct keys. Shape selection uses an explicit fixed sixteen-bucket mapping. The three original review keys retain their forms; other prototype keys may select a different body when newly placed. No signature/network migration is performed.

**Verified:** isolated Java 21 `build` and Kithkyn compatibility pass with **104 unit tests**, zero failures/errors/skips. All **12 selected Standing Stone world tests** pass, including sixteen forms in four facings, joined collision halves and all 36 finish recipes. The full world suite was not repeated for this model expansion. Author drift and independent geometry checks pass. The [package evidence](art/standing-stone-native-v4/verification.json) confirms all 48 captured meshes, 30 compiled travel classes and 1,887 generated resources match the reviewed jar. Unrelated preview capture jobs changed after the build; Standing Stone production assets and classes retain parity.

## October 6 · native player mana, Homebound Eye and shared nearby arrival

Implemented the accepted 100-mana player pool: full on first spawn and death/respawn, recovery of 2 mana per second after five seconds without a successful expenditure, and a synchronized HUD hidden whenever mana is full. Login and nondeath clones preserve the amount and remaining delay. Native casts and Gluttony use the same pool. Spell costs are unchanged; the 214-spell catalog retains its current balance policy.

Homebound Eye is a reusable 30-durability item bound to the crafting Spellstone’s dimension/address. Its four-inner-Plinth recipe consumes one valid Attunement Shard, Spider Eye, Ender Pearl and Gold Ingot, shapeless and with an empty center. The full shard key is copied unchanged. Only the Spider Eye’s Plinth imbuement selects payment: empty spends 6 durability (five returns), Soul Sand spends 3 hearts and 1 wear, Moss Block spends 3 hunger icons and 2 wear, Lapis Block spends 25 actual XP points and 2 wear, and Amethyst Block spends 30 mana and 2 wear. Resource affordability and destination validation precede the transaction; a canceled transfer refunds payment without wear. The fifth default return consumes the depleted Eye. Both optional recipe viewers expose the public device recipe.

Homebound Eye and Standing Stones share `NearbyTeleport`: randomized open, supported, dry positions within two horizontal blocks, allowing a one-block height step. Every candidate is checked before the owner-requested occupied fallback at the destination’s height. The helper never clears terrain. Missing destinations and unaffordable payments still reject. The Eye supports available dimensions; Standing Stone travel retains its separate same-dimension scope and unfinished payment system.

## October 6 · Standing Stone network and native map/list draft

The owner selected **Standing Stone**, matching Attunement Shard keys, **same-dimension travel only**, and a map with clickable destinations alongside a list. The [native draft](design/standing-stones.md) registers a saved travel device, world-owned peer directory and server-authoritative selection/arrival. A four-offering ritual consumes one valid shard, an Ender Pearl and two Chiseled Stone Bricks, copying the shard's existing key unchanged; this composition is a first authored recipe proposal. Active inputs use the existing atomic ritual lifecycle, outer nodes are ignored, and sockets remain installed. Mining retains the device's key/name; saved endpoints persist across chunk unload/restart and are revalidated when selected. Anvil names label destinations.

The native screen plots actual X/Z coordinates on a grid with synchronized pins/list, page/zoom controls and the existing rune signature. Same-dimension members are filtered before paging; requests to another dimension reject even with matching keys. Travel requires a live nearby source session, an exact-key destination and safe existing ground. Rejected, hazardous, obstructed, removed, foreign-key and replayed requests do not move the player. Travel uses native portal particles and sound, without changing terrain or keeping permanent destination chunk tickets.

**Verified:** Java 21 `build`, `verifyKithkynCompatibility` and `runGameTestServer` pass: **95 unit tests and all 158 required Minecraft behavior tests**, zero failures/errors/skips. New tests cover bound/name-preserving placement/drop data, saved membership, exact-key travel, source proximity, one-use sessions, same-dimension enforcement, hazardous/blocked/removed destinations, inactive outer offerings and atomic cancellation after only the shard's key changes. The initial run exposed an unrelated recipe-viewer sync attempting to send custom payloads to unnegotiated mock players; both viewer sync and the new network view now check channel support. The placement fixture also needed explicit air clearing. The full repaired run passes.

Four [actual Minecraft UI screenshots](art/standing-stone-network/README.md) were inspected at 640×360 and 480×270 GUI units, including real map-pin and eighth-row clicks. Native inspection exposed background blur and cramped marker labels; the final screen owns its backdrop and uses legible marker backplates. The archive retains unchanged framebuffer images, click checks and source hashes. These are menu fixtures, not teleportation gameplay recordings. Packaged travel classes match the latest inspected compile; [verification](art/standing-stone-network/verification.json) records the draft jar and checks.

The later accepted payment design uses the Pearl's Plinth imbuement, rather than a socket on the finished stone. Payment selection/charging, final Standing Stone models, device recipe-viewer entries and optional terrain maps remain outstanding. The later finish implementation replaces this draft's single Chiseled Stone Brick cube with 36 temporary vanilla-textured cubes. [Pinned Atlas research](research/standing-stone-maps.md) distinguishes Antique Atlas, Atlas 4/Surveyor, Map Atlases and the unrelated texture Atlas API; no map dependency or copied map assets are packaged. ## October 5 · submerged apparatus, offering clearance and independent column sockets

Every Plinth segment, including covered bases and shafts, retains an independently installable/recoverable side imbuement. Offering placement requires air or unobstructed fluid immediately above the Plinth. Ordinary solid covers—including waterlogged solid blocks—reject new ingredients and ritual participation, without deleting stored items; existing covered offerings remain recoverable. Placement prediction, direct insertion, rendering, node selection and atomic commitment use the same clearance predicate. Covering a reserved surface cancels before consumption and releases its locks.

Both Spellstone and Plinth implement vanilla waterlogging, across all 72 cosmetic apparatus blocks and all column parts. Survival placement in source/flowing water retains water; water-bucket filling/draining and column neighbor changes preserve the offering entity, items and embedded material. ## October 4 material Spellshaping and eight-slot Attunement Shards

Extra protection uses owned finite bindings. Supported projectile delivery parameters and finite lifetimes are bounded; saturated/no-op and incompatible routes reject before consumption. The original definitions, rarity, base costs and relative trait units remain immutable.

The **Attunement Shard requires all eight active slots**: one each of Amethyst Shard, Copper Ingot, Iron Ingot, Gold Ingot, Quartz, Lapis Lazuli, Redstone and Glowstone Dust, without a reference. Offerings are consumed once; installed materials stay. Its inspectable versioned blueprint retains relative positions, both shapes, independent heights and local ingredient–material pairings. ## October 4 native leyline system · initial implementation, superseded by cleanup above

This entry records the initial implementation milestone. Its migration policy, test counts and installation status are historical; the fresh-world cleanup above supersedes them.

The accepted v3 response matrices now drive actual Spellstone/Plinth ingredient crafting for all **214 spell recipes**. One Plinth type serves both rings; its material socket saves, synchronizes, renders and drops independently. Spellstone is half a block high, Plinth is 6/16, and both use standalone Chiseled Stone Brick/Iron construction. Old pedestal blocks migrate on load without losing offerings or duplicating drops. Their registry IDs remain hidden migration aliases, while their construction recipes are retired. Previous three-block art remains archived.

World selection finds the nearest complete regular Cross/Diagonal inner ring within radius 8, then valid outer candidates within radius 16 and independent signed height steps ±6. It never loads chunks. References select capacity; ingredients resolve a unique matching layout. Ambiguity rejects safely, eight-slot matches take precedence without a reference, and four-slot crafting ignores all outer nodes, sockets and offerings. Decorations and foundations have no numeric semantics. Atomic reservation, ingredient-only lifting, flat reference/result display and creature-only failure blasts remain intact.

Crafted scrolls persist Amplify, Range, Area and Casting Cost, derived from the output's complete configured descriptive trait set; reference bonuses do not stack. Real casts retain those values through recasts and lasting callbacks. Outcomes apply only at authored scaling consumers; unsupported axes do nothing and casting cost stays independent. Existing cost components compose before rounding and scale once; health costs round in hearts, damage/healing in HP, and ratios/velocities retain precision. Discovery weights and identification rules are unchanged, with fragment reconstruction producing unshaped base scrolls. Base definitions, rarity and numerical trait ratios are unchanged.

Actual Survival Heal verifies 6 HP, 17 mana and scroll consumption; spatial tests verify expanded Area and Range boundaries and rounded damage. **26 calculator/audit tests**, **11 ritual-authoring tests** and **17 spell-balance tests** pass. The regenerated **3,616,938-evaluation** numerical audit passes all guards. Packaged rules/model/recipe checks, both conversion workflows and spell-reference/balance/Pathfinder inventory checks pass. Kithkyn platform compatibility is verified; no foreign mod is required by the leyline runtime.

## October 4 beneficial specialization and minor offsets · calculator proposal only

The owner clarified that a matching structure should be largely beneficial for its output type, with minor balancing offsets. The preceding cost correction still allowed some maximum-Range or maximum-Area arrangements to sacrifice 20–30% of another outcome. The current shared response raises bounded sub-neutral outcome factors to **0.30**, retaining full positive gains and reducing the strongest losses to **8.3% Amplify / 10.1% Range and Area**. Positive combined cost logs now use **0.20** for smaller payment offsets; negative combined logs keep their full value. The separate geometry rows, both height steps and componentwise final-amount rounding remain intact.

Smaller outcome penalties also reduce tradeoff refunds. School Cost cells were therefore refitted offline toward minimum factors of 0.90 with four slots and 0.83 with eight, retaining better existing results. Dormant independent Cost cells are activated where required; Transmutation's Cost first-distance preference is 4 for a broader efficiency neighborhood. No school-specific evaluator branch is added. Every outcome matrix remains unchanged, and exact comparison of all 82 examples at both capacities confirms their Amplify/Range/Area maxima and winning layouts are preserved. **158 cost minima change**; the audit is regenerated instead of assuming prior efficiency results still hold.

Divination + Light's 33×33 pyramid gives **+23.8% Range for +6.7% Casting Cost**; 20 mana rounds to **21 mana**. Nature's 11×11 Life + Plant + Wood circle gives **+17.6% Amplify / +18.0% Area for +5.9% cost**, also 20 → **21 mana**. ## October 3 native scrolls and ritual crafting

The selected second Spellstone crown texture remains active. The shared apparatus now supports native scroll casting/identification, crafting-grid dismantling, fragment discovery and explicit ritual recipes for **all 214 spells**. Recipes occupy 139 four-slot, 14 five-slot, 44 six-slot, seven seven-slot and ten eight-slot layouts, with exactly one Paper ingredient. A retained center scroll supplies hints and failure risk but is optional for a correct arrangement. Incorrect unreferenced attempts reject safely; incomplete reference inspections never roll risk. Success consumes ingredients once and preserves the reference; vanilla containers return to their stands. Failed referenced rituals destroy every offering and damage nearby living creatures while preserving terrain, reference scrolls, loose items and decorations.

Native feedback uses actual item-shaped color-gradient shadows, steady correct-item glow, sideways shake and up/down movement toward the required pedestal type. Correct crafting raises all pedestals together and draws the recovered diamond/spoke/crossed connection graph before a scroll appears. These are server-backed operations with client movement; collision and world blocks do not move. Snapshot validation cancels a broken or changed structure before consumption; reference and crafted output persist separately. Paid scroll casts consume once, successful completion identifies, unpaid interruptions retain the item, and awaiting recasts continue the original paid cast through empty-hand use.

The owner's subsequent clarification reserves **slot-local supporting-block augmentation and spell shaping** for the next stage. Each base will affect the ingredient immediately above it. No provisional Diamond/Amethyst/Iron global boost is enabled; current foundations impose no recipe requirement or output multiplier. Unknown/volatile failure tuning retains the existing maximum policy; combined danger tuning, mana acquisition/regeneration, wands and equipment remain future design work.

Verification: `author_ritual_recipes.py --check` and all **11 authoring contract tests** pass, including every rotated recipe-pair intersection across all accepted alternatives. The native catalog test verifies all 214 baseline ingredients against the actual non-Air registry, all four rotations, and unambiguous standalone matches. **61 JUnit tests and 110 Minecraft GameTests pass** on the pinned baseline. World checks cover optional references, five/seven-part recipes, safe/incomplete attempts, atomic cancellation, full-offering destruction, living-creature radius and preserved terrain/drops, container returns, independent saves, no-RNG previews, discovery trait intersection, successful identification, failed payment, paid forfeit, reserved-item interruption, and once-only recast payment. Native spell definitions, base costs, rarities and balance policy are unchanged. Apparatus model/texture drift and Kithkyn platform checks pass.

Actual Minecraft footage for [hints, unreferenced success, mixed discovery and creature-only failure](art/ritual-crafting/README.md) is archived and visually inspected. Capture metadata records 256/100/100/102 server ticks, real framebuffer frames, model/texture hashes, crafted output and observed villager health. Both successful operations produced one native scroll. The failure kept the outside villager at 20 health and reduced the inside villager to approximately 12.11 while preserving its reference and terrain. The final build and native capture run pass.

The initial completed ritual `vestige-0.1.0.jar` was installed at **Kithkyn Testing → minecraft/mods** in Prism Launcher, replacing the earlier display-only jar with a recoverable backup outside `mods`. Installed and packaged SHA-256 match: `e13f08b659a71e289f04e4edd925871d7fa0e5b97dee54b6ee14472b103e541e`. [The installation record](art/ritual-crafting/prism-install.json) includes the actual instance's Minecraft 1.21.1 and NeoForge 21.1.248. Restart Minecraft to load it. ## Platform and scope

Minecraft **1.21.1**, NeoForge **21.1.72**, Java **21**, one Gradle project under `src/`. Development runs co-load the sibling Kithkyn JAR and check matching platform versions. Curios and inherited compatibility dependencies are removed.

The project owner originally deferred native wands, discovery, and progression and requested deletion of inherited systems. Native scroll discovery and crafting were subsequently authorized; wands and equipment remain deferred. The retired layer included wands/upgrades, books/workbench, discovery/XP tiers, legacy spell registry/casting, artifacts, world generation, recipes/loot, renderer/assets/sounds, attachments, networking, mixins, service-loader adapters, and data generators. The active source tree now contains the native spell framework and its bootstrap/tests. A local pre-removal working-tree backup was preserved; attribution remains in `CREDITS.md`/`LICENSE.md`.

The owner returned to progression design on October 2. The [scroll discovery specification](design/spell-discovery.md) records unknown/identified knowledge, identification only through successful scroll casts and dismantling into three independently weighted fragments. On October 3 the owner named the structure's center **Spellstone**, its four inner stands **Stone Pedestals**, and the four additional outer stands **Runic Pedestals**. Both forms retain the same Spellstone and inner stands; the advanced form has eight ingredient slots total. The accepted design lets each slot read its supporting block. Discovery accepts at least four fragments whose traits may differ, requires eligible spells to contain every supplied trait, then weights them by their average rating across fragment slots, counting duplicates. This replaces matching-only discovery and the separate-station proposal. [Iron's material properties and flavor](design/material-crafting.md) inspire distinct ingredient contributions, with 37 frozen references; the owner rejects generic material-quality scaling and follows patterns/parts only loosely. The owner subsequently authorized [native apparatus construction and item display](design/apparatus-models.md), now implemented with the selected recipes and a redesigned Spellstone. The owner then authorized native scroll casting/identification, fragment discovery, structure validation, all 214 ritual recipes and their feedback. Those operations are implemented as described in [Spellstone rituals](design/ritual-crafting.md). Supporting-block spell shaping and equipment remain future work: the block directly beneath each pedestal will augment the contribution of that individual ingredient, with exact mappings and combination rules still open. Current forfeit tuning also lacks the requested combined danger for unknown volatile spells.

The owner also confirmed that Iron's established material standards and flavor apply in standalone Vestige. Required apparatus and core progression must have vanilla/native acquisition routes without any Iron mod. Installed Iron mods should supply optional ingredient/material alternatives usable directly, while standalone recipes and vanilla material meanings remain intact. This does not require cloning Iron's material catalog. Spell ritual recipes now accept 15 verified optional Iron item IDs alongside 54 distinct vanilla baseline ingredients. Installing Iron is not a prerequisite; provider-installed behavior still needs separate playtesting. Slot-local augmentation consumers remain future work; the current build and mod metadata declare no Iron dependency.

## Implemented

- **Three native apparatus blocks:** Spellstone, Stone Pedestal and Runic Pedestal, with the selected vanilla-material recipes, Creative Functional Blocks entries, shaped collision, pickaxe mining, loot and recipe unlocks. Each stores, saves and synchronizes one resting item; right-click places it, empty-hand right-click retrieves it, and replacement drops it safely. The [implementation and actual Minecraft screenshots](design/apparatus-models.md) cover the fully redesigned Spellstone and unchanged accepted pedestals. Native discovery and ritual crafting are now implemented; ingredient shaping remains planned.
- **110 explicit native adaptations** of the default-enabled Iron catalog at pinned commit `e4056af90302d37eb1739f5ff05020b020e6e252`. Every definition retains exact source spell/school identity and revision, and executes without Iron installed. The [conversion ledger](design/iron-spell-conversions.md) records each recipe and its differences.
- **100 Pathfinder 2e native adaptations**, including 20 source cantrips and spells through rank 10. Frozen AoN IDs, book/pages, traditions, errata, rank/cantrip and source rarity are recorded separately from native tuning. [The conversion ledger](design/pathfinder-spell-conversions.md) documents exact behavior and deliberate differences; no tabletop rules engine or source assets are imported. New reusable typed damage, undead-tag conditions, include-self queries and finite existing-mob control support distinct spell behavior.
- A pinned [1,992-entry Pathfinder inventory](pathfinder-spell-inventory.md) across 102 publication labels and [32 additional directly reviewed candidates](research/pathfinder-visual-library-batch.md), grouped into eight gameplay families with exact source references, proposed adaptations and reusable presentation needs. Sixteen reviewed sources now have native definitions; sixteen remain reviewed candidates.
- The [100-source Pathfinder selection](pathfinder-spell-selection.md) is now fully converted. The latest 36 add shared native constructs, physical scale and traversal, consensual formations, reactive/shared protection, acoustic/perception volumes, private sensors, conservative stone editing, loose-metal movement and identity-preserving pet shelter. Every source has an explicit recipe, matching policy and adaptation notes; earlier proposals are historical.
- Every spell authors animated presentation: **214 spells** across thirty-two shared shape families, optional vanilla sounds, bounded server/client cue lifetimes and private observers. Stealth renewal stays visually silent, and cosmetic RNG does not alter gameplay RNG. Geometry is original. New camera/movement/perception leases are separate from cosmetic art and remain server authoritative.
- The [cast gallery](effects-workshop.md) includes actual Minecraft primary-cast recordings for **all 214 spells**, opens the full catalog, and offers MP4 download and native cast commands. Captions record observed creature health, displacement, status and world changes. Browser sketches, isolated effect studies, canvas recording and Three.js are removed. The developer-only `/vestige_magic effects` diagnostic remains available in Minecraft.

- Explicit **common, uncommon, rare, and mythic** rarities for all 214 spells: 51 common, 89 uncommon, 64 rare, and 10 mythic. Rarity remains immutable through trait resolution and is queryable as a text condition fact. Older definitions and constructor signatures default to common.
- [Relative trait ratings and boost math](design/spell-balance.md), with a [complete catalog audit](spell-balance-audit.md). Raw ratings and totals do not measure power or determine rarity. The audit records baseline parameters, individual and combined boost responses, symbolic world facts, and spatial coverage; gameplay balance compares actual outcomes and constraints. Interposing Earth's original descriptive profile is restored; its protection plan and relative units are preserved; its native cost is now tuned.
- [Full native gameplay balance pass](spell-balance-review.md) across all 214 spells: explicit roles, coefficients, mana, charge and cooldown costs, control/protection charges and durations, summon health/lifetimes, target limits, and weapon contributions. Shared hit caps prevent repeated dash/volley/meteor hits from multiplying output. Fields have deterministic elapsed pulses; same-spell summons replace their backing cohorts.
- A sixteen-spell Pathfinder expansion adds shattering wards, supported local/random teleportation, harmless decoy/lure variants, end-on-damage invisibility, finite slowing patches, reversed Heal/Harm eligibility, private magic inspection, capped cloud/swarm pulses, a combat wolf, and once-only continuously sampled occupancy healing. New reusable actions keep these mechanics available to future recipes.
- Four additional native examples: Force Arrow, Summon Zombie, Arcane Lock, Interposing Earth. There are **214 runtime spell definitions** in total.
- Immutable spells, four traditions, flat open traits, explicit numerical scaling, derived capabilities, costs, conditions, cast modes, charge/channel plans, target anchors, numerical snapshots, recasts, temporary event bindings, and owned manifestations.
- Native targeting for rays, beams, cones, chains, nearby/aimed areas, owned/allied creatures, and saved subjects; bounded distance, collision, and line-of-sight handling.
- Reusable projectile impacts, homing/piercing/rain/spread, held-item recovery, summon cohorts and dismissal, following/persistent fields, tethers, two-point walls/portals, status effects, healing, mining, movement, inventory access, and private rooms.
- Real temporary block formation through `block_wall`: staggered Minecraft ice models rise with frost/chip particles and safely lift creatures before becoming colliding, breakable terrain. Wall of Ice creates fifteen cells at baseline, remains for ten seconds and retracts only surviving spell-owned cells. Broken cells and player replacements remain changed; conjured ice supplies no loot or meltwater. Immediate cleanup and scheduled orphan checks prevent permanent abandoned ice.
- Before-commit damage/healing interception and causal event dispatch. Heartstop accumulates prevented damage and repays half on expiry; Echoing Strikes dispatches on the attacker and excludes magic follow-ups; Blight suppresses incoming healing.
- Standalone native projectile/field backing entities and client renderers. Operator `cast` is the free development entrypoint; `cast_balanced` enforces native mana/cooldowns and `mana` controls the 200-energy test seam. Recasts share payment/recovery, and only one charge/channel per actor can execute at once.
- Updated README, architecture instructions, runtime design, glossary, credits, and historical scope notices. The September 14 integration-only Iron decision is superseded for native content conversion; future foreign-cast interoperability remains separate work.
- [Complete spell reference](spell-reference.md) covering all 214 definitions, with worked examples, trait ratings and actual reads, authored costs, targeting, effect plans, current appearance, and adaptation notes. `python3 tools/document_spells.py --check` checks its generated catalog against the definitions and authoring notes.

## Current art pass

Every spell now has an explicit main silhouette, secondary movement, material, density and rhythm in [the art ledger](spell-art-direction.md). Sixteen new shared shapes supplement the original sixteen: coiling strands, runes, crystals, stems, surging flames, raised singularities, comets, leaves, rays, pressure rings, crescents, chains, wings, teeth, clocks and eyes. Protocol 4 carries bounded speed, phase and count values. Original geometry and vanilla assets remain independent of traits and gameplay tuning. A comparison against the previous 214 definitions confirms identical executable plans, targeting, relative traits, rarity and costs after removing cosmetics.

The actual-render review found short projectiles with no hit feedback, duplicated hero effects at field tick recipients, first-person flight hiding its wings, and undersized rooted stems. Shared impact cues, field ownership, a third-person flight fixture and longer organic curves address those findings. The Gluttony fixture now holds normal client eat input: a real bread item is consumed and observed mana changes from zero to thirty. The food-to-mana action displays the actual gain after its 200-mana cap. A world test exercises four actual meals and verifies that only the first three grant mana.

All **214 replacement primary-cast videos** are now published in the gallery, with new movie hashes and matching current definition bytes. Actual-frame contact sheets cover the full catalog; individual sequences were inspected for blood/teeth, wings, frost, fire impacts, water pressure, singularities, rooted growth, wards, companions and information/movement utilities. The review also added finite contact feedback to default projectile damage, reduced Planar Sight's oversized cosmetic form and ended Water Walk's scripted crossing at the far bank. Gluttony shows a real consumed bread and the “+30 mana” actionbar. Unique data is a coverage check, not a substitute for judging the footage. Wands, discovery and progression remain deferred.

### October 2 shared structure and mixed-fragment discovery

Recorded and explicitly locked the owner's accepted discovery design: basic four-slot and advanced eight-slot forms, center piece and per-slot supporting-block influence. Discovery accepts one fragment in each of at least four outer slots, with any trait combination, and intersects the enabled spell catalog by positive presence of every supplied trait. All Fire inputs can yield a Fire-only or Fire + Evocation spell; mixed Fire and Evocation inputs exclude Fire-only spells. The owner chose arithmetic-average weights across all participating fragment slots; duplicates count repeatedly. Four Fire fragments retain the original Fire-rating odds, while three Fire plus one Evocation uses `(3 × fire + evocation) / 4`. This is an accepted design, not executable station behavior. Supporting-block mappings/effects, center function, geometry, activation and crafting/augmentation recipes remain open.

Checked intersection eligibility and exact probability examples, including duplicate counts, missing-trait exclusion, fractional ratings, the original 5:1 rule and equal composition across four/eight slots. Local links and whitespace pass. No runtime code, spell definitions, material reference or media changed, so gameplay/client suites were not rerun. These checks verify the recorded math rather than implement discovery.

The capture creature keeps vanilla falling physics while its movement speed prevents wandering. Frame inspection confirms staggered rising ice models, frost/chip bursts, the creature on the finished wall, descending/shattering ice and the creature back on the ground afterward. The in-app viewer decodes the refreshed cast at 960×540, ready state 4, with no media error; the [workshop screenshot](previews/wall-of-ice-blocks-workshop.png) records the selected actual cast. The recording is silent and uses the operator resource bypass. Broken/replaced-cell behavior is exercised by world tests; the clip depicts an intact wall. Multiplayer viewing, adversarial terrain changes during lifting, and restart recovery in ordinary saved worlds remain further playthrough checks.

### October 2 preview playback recovery

The local Vite preview server had stopped: no process listened on port 5175, and video requests failed to connect. Restarting it in an independent background session restored the preview. All 322 video URLs now return HTTP 200 with the correct MP4 content type and matching recorded SHA-256 hashes; byte-range requests return HTTP 206 with the correct lengths and bytes. The in-app browser decodes and plays both the actual Wall of Ice cast and its native effect replay at 960×540, with ready state 4 and no media error. The working preview is left open. The local server still needs to run while using the viewer; recordings remain saved in the project. No runtime, spell definition or media bytes changed, so unit/world suites were not rerun for this server recovery.

### October 1 actual Minecraft footage

The [workshop](effects-workshop.md) now ships **322 native videos: 314 effect-phase replays covering all 214 definitions, plus eight real cast examples**. The longer casts cover Electric Arc, Fireball, Freezing Rain, Mirror Image, Protector Tree, Summon Animal, Wall of Ice and Wall of Water. Seven plans complete; Summon Animal correctly remains awaiting its dismissal recast. Native timing, targeting and backing entities execute unchanged, with NPC fixtures and the development resource/recovery bypass. Recordings are excerpts, not complete lifecycle or specialized-encounter coverage. They are silent 960×540 H.264; actual sampled timestamps are retained when encoding into a 30 fps container.

Native frame inspection exposed and corrected a double camera rotation and washed-out additive surfaces. The renderer uses the stage pose matrix and standard translucent quads. Construct replay fixtures retain the authored dimensions. Representative frames were inspected across all thirteen shape families, and real cast frames show target damage/ignition, physical panels, caster-following illusions, the rain field and a live summoned wolf. This establishes native pixel appearance for representative cases, not exhaustive gameplay or multiplayer correctness. The first replay batch can include Minecraft's chat strip; the final capture path exports after level rendering and before GUI rendering.

`./gradlew --offline prepareEffectsCapture build` passes with **56 JUnit tests**, zero failures/errors/skips. The added catalog test compares every browser phase with native duration/radius, ordered shapes/colors and construct dimensions. **Four Python capture checks** guard against stale definitions, changed video bytes, duplicate/out-of-range phases and incomplete coverage. `encode_native_capture.py --check --complete` verifies all videos, definitions and phase coverage. Media probing confirms all 322 files have one H.264 video stream at 960×540 / 30 fps, no audio stream, and container durations within 0.074 seconds of their recorded elapsed timestamps. **Five browser tests** and the TypeScript/Vite build pass. The 214-spell visual export and generated spell reference remain current. The earlier **74 required GameTests** remain the latest world-mechanics suite; this change adds an optional isolated scene and corrects client rendering, so that suite was not rerun.

The initial complete recording runs exported all frames but Gradle reported a configuration-cache problem in the setup task after capture. Resolving the game directory during configuration fixes it; the final setup/build and an isolated follow-up capture complete cleanly. Raw frames/timestamps remain in ignored build directories, encoded footage and provenance are in the viewer. File hashes confirm spell definitions still match their recordings. Browser inspection verifies native MP4 playback and the separation between actual casts, native phase replays and browser sketches. Detailed source rules, custom creature/weapon artwork and multiplayer observer/private-camera transitions remain explicit limits. Native wands, discovery and progression remain deferred.

`./gradlew --offline build runGameTestServer` passed with **54 JUnit tests** (zero failures/errors/skips) and **all 74 required Minecraft GameTests passed**. Nineteen additional encounters cover solid geometry and cleanup, one-time fracture, protection/shared damage pools, finite object utility, physical scale, wall/water contact, acoustic/privacy gates, vulnerable remote-view body, time-absence activation, projectile drag, boundary movement, support budgets, actual pet dimension travel and refused-insertion recovery, terrain restoration/occupant evacuation, conservative permanent stone movement, atomic ally formations and conditional breathing rescue. The tests target reusable mechanics; they do not exhaustively playtest all 214 spells or multiplayer combinations.

Both converters and the spell-reference, balance-audit/review, Pathfinder selection/inventory and animation-export checks pass. The selection has 100 unique implemented canonical identities and no pending selected recipe. **17 Python balance tests**, **3 presentation tests** and **3 browser catalog tests** pass. The TypeScript/Vite production build passes. Browser inspection covers search, phase selection, native command copying, playback/timeline, responsive mobile layout and real canvas recording/project save. The retired browser Wall of Ice sketch (removed October 2) is approximately eight seconds, H.264, 932×352, silent; it is browser recipe footage. A screenshot records the completed tool. Minecraft client startup co-loads Kithkyn and initializes resources, atlas and audio without a new presentation error. Desktop access was initially locked, then became available, but computer-use inventory did not expose the development Java/Minecraft window (`Minecraft` and `java` app selection failed). Actual game appearance, client prediction and observer transitions were therefore not visually inspected or recorded. The browser reproduces recipe geometry; it does not prove native pixel appearance. Wands/discovery/progression remain deferred.

The verification entries below describe earlier milestones and counts; this entry supersedes their current-catalog and proposal status.

### October 1 hundred-source selection

The selection contains **100 distinct canonical Pathfinder sources: 64 native recipes and 36 new reviewed proposals**. The latter cover construction, allied positioning, reactive/shared protection, body geometry, private information, boundaries, objects and companions. New sources were directly checked on AoN; canonical remaster aliases are used, while three explicitly legacy spells retain their edition. Each proposal identifies a meaningful difference from its closest existing native counterparts. Unsupported source behaviors are recorded as missing mechanics, not relabeled generic buffs or attacks.

Both converters were regenerated. `document_pathfinder_selection.py --check` validates all 100 unique canonical IDs/names, all 36 complete proposals, accepted trait names, comparison links and exact agreement with the 64 implemented definitions. Existing Pathfinder conversion, inventory, spell-reference, balance-audit and balance-review checks pass, as do all **15 Python balance tests**. Both changed generators compile. Local file links in all nine changed Markdown documents resolve. A before/after SHA-256 comparison confirms all **178 native definitions**, the **64-source implementation ledger** and the **178-entry balance policy** remain byte-for-byte unchanged.

This was collection/documentation work; no new runtime mechanic, final balance assignment, visual asset or gameplay result is claimed for a proposed spell. Gradle and Minecraft behavior suites were not rerun because their code/data did not change. The prior 54 JUnit / 55 GameTest verification remains recorded below. The 100-source collection is a selected total, not 100 additional native spells.

### October 1 sixteen-spell Pathfinder expansion

The second source batch adds sixteen explicit definitions for **178 total spells**, including **64 Pathfinder adaptations**. All sixteen have shared presentation data. Costs/rarities are authored independently of source rank: five common, ten uncommon and one rare. All previous 162 definition files remain byte-for-byte unchanged. The [conversion ledger](design/pathfinder-spell-conversions.md) and [source research](research/pathfinder-expansion-batch.md) record the behavior, exact references and adaptation limits.

`./gradlew --offline build runGameTestServer` passed with **54 JUnit tests** (zero failures/errors/skips) and **all 55 required Minecraft GameTests passed**. Both converters and all generated reference/audit/review/inventory checks pass, along with **15 Python balance tests**. All 178 packaged JSON definitions match their source bytes, and all previous 162 spell files are byte-for-byte preserved. Local Markdown file links in the changed documents resolve. World fixtures use immediate copied definitions and explicit event positions for outcome/lifecycle isolation; existing paid casting tests retain real resource/recovery coverage. The new checks cover shattering mitigation/retaliation, safe versus unsupported landing, harmless one-hit lures, invisibility release/dispel/external-effect preservation, ground-field strength/expiry, living/undead outcome reversal and amplification, native/equipped magic sensing, finite cloud pulses, captured swarm targets, wolf stats/recast dismissal, limited mitigation/random teleportation and continuous dwell reset/eligibility.

Vanilla taming initially overwrote authored wolf health/attack; applying those values after taming fixes both the combat wolf and one-hit illusion proxy. Following fields now stop if their captured creature changes dimensions. Invisibility stops its two-tick refresh instead of removing an unrelated longer external effect. Dwell observes recipients every five ticks: leave/reentry wholly between samples cannot be detected.

These adaptations are intentionally finite approximations: no full tabletop action variants/rosters, player-specific illusion disbelief, real slippery terrain, metal-cloud expansion, full item identification, private camera or distinct new creature artwork. Desktop access to the game window remains unavailable; the new appearance and multiplayer observer behavior were not visually inspected. Wands/discovery/progression remain deferred.

### Pathfinder batch verification

```bash
./gradlew --offline build runGameTestServer
python3 tools/convert_irons_spells.py
python3 tools/convert_pathfinder_spells.py
python3 tools/convert_pathfinder_spells.py --check

```

The final build and packaging passed with **45 JUnit tests** (zero failures/errors/skips), **all 39 required Minecraft GameTests passed**, and **13 Python tests passed**. The packaged JAR contains all 162 spell definitions and matches the authored JSON exactly. All 162 native role/cost/rarity entries agree with generated definitions; all 114 previous definition files are byte-for-byte preserved. Local Markdown file references resolve.

The thirteen new world checks verify two distinct Electric Arc targets, living/undead eligibility, finite personal Shield, ordinary-versus-fire resistance, typed spell classification without ordinary-attack echoes, attacker-directed Fire Shield retaliation, delayed regeneration and fire interruption, existing-undead control expiry/dispel, overlapping control leases, indiscriminate life-field healing including the caster and undead damage, Hydraulic Push displacement, and Spiritual Armament’s stored target and final cleanup. The spirit weapon uses the original creature anchor rather than damaging its visual backing. Life-field fixtures explicitly disable AI and give their skeleton a helmet; daylight burning and ranged AI are external damage sources rather than field output.

Source metadata retains book/page, exact AoN ID/URL, displayed errata, traditions, source rank/cantrip flag and source rarity. Native rarity, cost and outcome tuning are independent, with descriptive relative units of one for this batch. Balance reviews compare mutually exclusive outcome branches separately, preserving the life field’s eligible damage/healing ceilings rather than summing incompatible cases. The shared policy is the sole authoring authority for costs, rarity and role reviews; effect recipes remain source-specific, with common helpers and no fallback.

This is the first 48-spell Pathfinder batch, not the full Pathfinder catalog or tabletop rules parity. All definitions parse and derive capabilities; targeted world encounters cover new mechanics and the existing suite, not every combination across every spell. Custom presentation, unsupported source rule details, wider PvP/AI/terrain/multiplayer playtesting and the normal-world private-dimension persistence checks remain future work. Wands, discovery and progression remain deferred.

### September 30 complete native balance pass

```bash
./gradlew --offline build runGameTestServer
python3 tools/test_spell_balance.py
```

The final build and packaging passed with **43 JUnit tests**, no failures/errors/skips, and **all 26 required Minecraft GameTests passed**. **Eleven Python tests** pass. The expanded world suite confirms tuned Sunbeam throughput and doubled-amplify damage, single-hit dash contact, six-target Fireball crowds, paid mana/cooldown rejection, summon stats/replacement, shared volley/meteor hit caps, exactly six healing-circle pulses, and no healing from overkill/corpses. Rain fixtures explicitly enable sky access; the default GameTest barrier roof correctly obstructs downward delivery.

All 114 role/cost entries match their definitions. Generated per-creature ceilings include pulse counts, all projectile deliveries and shared hit caps; they explicitly assume perfect delivery, eligible targets, no mitigation/overheal and uninterrupted execution. They exclude AI, terrain, status damage, conditional death/event chains, and utility from a scalar power score. This completes the initial native catalog tuning; future encounter playtesting can refine the baseline.

### Standalone catalog gameplay milestone

The following passed against the existing dependency cache:

```bash
./gradlew --offline build verifyKithkynCompatibility runGameTestServer
```

- **33 JUnit tests passed**, no failures/errors/skips. Complete catalog coverage checks all 110 source IDs, native definitions, provenance, and derived capabilities.
- **17 required Minecraft tests passed**, including projectile damage, healing, deferred repayment, causal echoes, healing suppression, full summon cohorts/recast dismissal, one-hit wards, counterspell interruption, persistent lock ownership, mining, sheep targeting, held-item recovery, linked portal transfer, persistent field damage, authored backing health/destruction cleanup, and private-room ownership/return behavior.
- Build, source/Javadoc packaging, and Kithkyn platform-version check passed. The JAR contains the standalone native catalog and delivery/backing entities, without inherited gameplay or mixin/service resources.
- The development client completed mod loading, resource loading, sound-engine startup, and texture-atlas initialization. This is startup verification, not a visual review or multiplayer playthrough of all spells.

GameTestServer intentionally bakes only its flat preset’s vanilla dimensions. The private-room test therefore checks the registered private dimension type and runs the same placement/ownership/return logic against its loaded End world. A normal-world private-dimension playthrough and restart persistence remain release checks.

Kithkyn reports that its local LLM runtime is unavailable in this test environment. Mod co-loading works; LLM functionality and spell interoperability are outside this milestone.

JUnit reports: `build/reports/tests/test/`. Artifact: `build/libs/vestige-0.1.0.jar`. All changes remain in the local working tree alongside the existing platform migration.

## Next work

The implemented catalog has **214 spells: 110 Iron adaptations, 100 Pathfinder adaptations and four native examples**, all with connected presentation. The [Pathfinder candidate inventory](pathfinder-spell-inventory.md) retains 1,992 frozen source entries; candidates remain separate from executable recipes. Current cast coverage and limitations are recorded above.

1. Review the 214 actual NPC casts with the owner, then refine dedicated spell silhouettes, creature/weapon artwork and character gestures. Add alternative-mode and specialized encounters involving targets, inventories, consent and reactions. Verify crowded scenes, observer transitions and visual clarity with multiple clients. Refine balance through PvP, terrain and AI encounters.
2. Playtest the implemented [two-block leyline system](leyline-playtesting.md), especially crowded neighboring apparatus and multi-client interaction. Author material-specific contributions for the working sockets and separately resolve stronger combined unknown/volatile risk. Equipment, passive/free-item balancing and sustainable energy design remain future work.
3. Verify reload/restart behavior, dimension entry/return, active-effect serialization policy, multiplayer, and optional-mod combinations before release.
4. Implement optional foreign Iron/Create/Kithkyn adapters independently of native content conversion.

## October 9 · approved Wardweave imbuements implemented

The owner approved [the complete Wardweave package](design/wardweave-imbuement-review.md): Warded, Enduring, Quickened and Reinforced independently compose into sixteen named sets. The unchanged eight-offering recipe reads retained local material sockets; matching wool determines dye. Duplicate selections and recognized unsupported riders reject before consumption. Stored selections are bounded trusted IDs, with source-local Force/Time/Amplify and typed recovery factors; no per-item executable data or new trait semantics are introduced. Reinforced uses 120 durability / 30 Callous Thread repair, and the other sets retain 80/20. Existing plain robes and public plain-color display IDs stay valid.

Actual matcher tests cover 4,480 color/rotation/socket layouts and all 256 public viewer patterns. All sixteen values, wearer composition, invalid saved selections/carrier budgets, actual charge/protected wear, snapshots/expiration, swaps/in-place edits, breaks, dye/repair/rename/save/load and atomic socket cancellation are verified. Full local verification passed 221 model tests in 48 suites and 385 native Minecraft tests with Kithkyn co-loaded. The generated adjective catalog passes its drift check. [Native inventory captures](art/wardweave-imbuements-native/README.md) verify all translated names and italic prefixes, unchanged loaded art, real protected wear, zero mana shortage and private wearer-owned recovery through expiry. The first client attempt hit vanilla login invulnerability and was replaced by the successful waiting capture. [The implementation receipt](verification/wardweave-implementation-2026-10-09.json) records the executed scope and source hashes.

This scoped publication preserves the baseline Cinderweave package; that chat's newer implementation is being combined separately without overwriting shared edits. Crane Bag's already-selected survival recipe follows. Balance in extended survival play and live multiplayer remain playtests.

## October 9 · Crane Bag survival construction and native viewers

The owner-selected Attunement Shard, Eye of Ender, Feather and Leather now produce one Crane Bag through the existing four-offering ritual. The relative cyclic order is Shard → Eye → Feather → Leather, with whole quarter-turns accepted. Wrong order, wrong layer, stacked inputs and unbound/forged shards reject. Construction copies the source's exact full key instead of recomputing it; added access points retain the original one-stack shared pool, contents and controls. There are no new carrier imbuement effects or changes to approved artwork in this pass.

The shared atomic ritual snapshots consume the four offerings once, retain embedded materials and cancel safely when the shard changes. Executed tests include two rotated crafts against one existing seven-diamond pool, duplicate activation, unchanged shared contents, cancellation and rejection before locks/consumption. The full local build passed 224 model tests in 49 suites and all 389 Minecraft tests with Kithkyn co-loaded, plus packaging and compatibility. [Actual JEI and EMI captures](art/crane-bag-recipe-native/README.md) show the live four-slot recipe and verify exactly one lookup for generic and bound outputs. [The recipe verification receipt](verification/crane-bag-construction-2026-10-09.json) records source/package hashes and the executed boundary.

Wardweave PR #9 has passed both GitHub checks and merged into main as ee9e4374. Its verified JAR was installed in Kithkyn Testing with every unrelated prior JAR member preserved. This subsequent Crane Bag build includes that Wardweave package and preserves baseline Cinderweave; the other robe chat owns the newer Cinder implementation and its shared-root integration. Crane Bag imbuements still need an approved package; any capacity change must define safe behavior for all bags sharing one key before it is implemented. Extended survival/multiplayer playtesting remains follow-up.
